### 15.2 `R24B-E1` `GLC_Graph_v3` Learned Semiring Edge-Pointer Gate on Clean `CLRS-30` ($N=16 \to 32 \to 64$, `4x` OOD, `196.66s` on `NVIDIA L4` GPU, `[R24B:1-84, JR24B, GV3]`)

Wave-2 (`R22B-E2`, `Section 13.2`) exposed a clean architectural trade-off on 100% zero-leakage `CLRS-30` ($N=16 \to 32 \to 64$, `4x` OOD): `GLC_AOM_v2`'s path-cumulative tropical relaxation (`d_uv + d_uw`) ranked **#1 of 5 on shortest-path relaxation (`Dijkstra` `0.8441` and `Bellman_Ford` `0.8356` Micro-F1 at $N=64$)**, but its fixed path-accumulation bias hurt single-edge greedy selection (`MST_Prim` `0.6416` vs. `0.8465` for `Triplet_GMPNN`) and level-synchronous `BFS` (`0.8053` vs. `0.9327`), placing `GLC_AOM_v2` `#5 of 5` in 6-task macro Micro-F1 (`0.7106 ± 0.0050`).

To resolve this without task-ID routing or hand-coded rules (`[GV3:1-215]`), **`GLC_Graph_v3`** introduces:
1. **Multi-Head Learned Continuous Semiring Gate ($\gamma_{t,h} \in [0, 1]$):** At each message-passing step $t$ and head $h \in \{1,\dots,4\}$, a lightweight node-edge summary gate predicts a continuous path-accumulation discount $\gamma_{t,h} = \sigma(w_{\gamma,h}^\top \bar{z}_t + b_{\gamma,h}) \in [0, 1]$ forming the generalized tropical edge message $m_{u \to v}^{(t,h)} = \gamma_{t,h} \cdot s_u^{(t,h)} + e_{uv}^{(t,h)}$, which smoothly interpolates between **tropical path accumulation ($\gamma \to 1$: `BFS`, `Bellman-Ford`, `Dijkstra`)** and **local single-edge greedy comparison ($\gamma \to 0$: `MST-Prim`, `DFS`, `Articulation Points`)**.
2. **Edge-Latent Bilinear Pointer Head ($\alpha_{\text{ptr}} \in [0, 1]$):** Combines final edge-message representations $E_{uv}^{(L)}$ with learned semiring relaxation logits via a task-adaptive blend gate $\alpha_{\text{ptr}}$ and degree-invariant log-degree normalization.

Executed on an **`NVIDIA L4` GPU** (`torch 2.14.1+cu130`, 3 seeds `101, 102, 103`, `196.66s` total wall-clock for `R24B-E1 + R24B-E2`, `0` AST `v2` violations, `7/7` canaries caught, `0.000000000` `PoisonTensor` max diff), **`GLC_Graph_v3` jumps from `#5` to `#1 Overall` across all 6 architectures at $N=64$ (`4x` OOD)** (`R24B-E1a` `PASS` and `R24B-E1b` `PASS`).

### 15.3 `R24B-E2` `GLC_AOM_v3` Linear-CFR Infoset Regret Memory on Pure-Neural `OpenSpiel` (`181,250` Params, `196.66s` on `NVIDIA L4` GPU, `[R24B:85-131, JR24B, GV3]`)

In Wave-2 (`R22A`, `Section 12.2`), scaling deterministic test-time tree search on `leduc_poker` from $B=1 \to 64$ *increased* `GLC_AOM_v2`'s `NashConv` from `4.9001` to `6.2167` because deterministic tree rollouts concentrate probability mass onto pure actions and collapse mixed-strategy entropy in 2-round imperfect-information poker (`R15-C4` theorem). To unify `GLC-AOM` with counterfactual regret minimization without external tabular solvers or extra parameters (`181,250` parameters across all 10 paradigms), **`GLC_AOM_v3`** (`[GV3:218-360]`) equips the `GLC-AOM` trunk with a dual-head `InfosetAdvantageHead` ($r_\theta(I, a)$, $\bar{\sigma}_\theta(I, a)$) and performs **Test-Time Linear-CFR Regret-Matching Updates** across $b = 1, \dots, B$ stochastic infoset passes:
$$R^{(b)}(I, a) = \max\!\Big(0,\; \frac{b-1}{b}\, R^{(b-1)}(I, a) + r_\theta^{(b)}(I, a)\Big), \qquad \bar{\sigma}^{(B)}(I, a) \propto \sum_{b=1}^{B} b \cdot \sigma^{(b)}(I, a).$$

Table 35 reports the 10-paradigm $\times$ 4-budget ($B \in \{1, 4, 16, 64\}$) pure-neural `OpenSpiel` shootout across `kuhn_poker` (`12` infosets), `leduc_poker` (`936` infosets), `phantom_ttt(obstype=reveal-nothing)` (`108` infosets), and `dark_hex(board_size=2)` (`134` infosets):
- **Massive Improvement over `GLC_AOM_v2` (`p = 0.0070`) & `R24B-E2b` (`PASS`):** `GLC_AOM_v3` reduces 4-game mean `NashConv` at $B=64$ from `1.8701 ± 0.0651` (`GLC_AOM_v2`) to **`1.5798 ± 0.0589`** (**`-0.2903` reduction**, paired $t = -11.92, p = 0.0070$; **`1.5635 ± 0.0636` at $B=4$**, **`1.5760 ± 0.0607` at $B=16$**), while `GLC_AOM_v3_Generalist` drops from `1.7967 ± 0.0427` (`v2_Generalist`) to **`1.6044 ± 0.1094`** at $B=64$ (**`1.5964 ± 0.1606` at $B=4$**). Unlike `GLC_AOM_v2` (where $B=1 \to 64$ degraded `leduc_poker` `4.9001 -> 6.2167`), both `GLC_AOM_v3` (`1.6284 -> 1.5635 -> 1.5760 -> 1.5798`) and `GLC_AOM_v3_Generalist` (`1.6804 -> 1.5964 -> 1.6028 -> 1.6044`) improve from $B=1 \to 64$ (`R24B-E2b` `PASS`).
- **Closing `91.4%` of the Gap to `Deep_CFR` (`R24B-E2a` `PARTIAL PASS / STATISTICAL TIE`):** At $B=64$, `GLC_AOM_v3` (`1.5798 ± 0.0589`) closes **`91.4%` (`0.2903 / 0.3160`)** of the 4-game `NashConv` gap between `GLC_AOM_v2` (`1.8701`) and `Deep_CFR` (`1.5541 ± 0.1745`), landing within **`+0.0257` `NashConv` (`t = +0.25, p = 0.8237`, statistically indistinguishable)** with **`3.0x` lower cross-seed standard deviation** (`0.0589` vs. `0.1745`) and beating **`R_NaD` (`1.7160 ± 0.0631`) by `-0.1362`**, **`AlphaZero_PUCT` (`1.8234`) by `-0.2436`**, **`Gumbel_MuZero` (`1.8238`) by `-0.2440`**, **`PPO_GAE` (`2.1222`) by `-0.5423`**, and **`GRPO` (`2.1657`) by `-0.5859`**. On `kuhn_poker` at $B=4$, **`GLC_AOM_v3_Generalist` (`0.4028 ± 0.0573`) beats `Deep_CFR` (`0.4270 ± 0.0442`) for #1 overall**; on `phantom_ttt` ($B=64$) and `dark_hex` ($B=64$), both `GLC_AOM_v3_Generalist` (**`0.3804 ± 0.1456`** and **`0.5918 ± 0.2119`**) and `GLC_AOM_v3` (**`0.4484 ± 0.0462`** and **`0.6520 ± 0.0572`**) beat `Deep_CFR` (`0.4710 ± 0.0907` and `0.7781 ± 0.5022`). Because `1.5798` sits `+0.0257` above `1.5541`, we report `R24B-E2a` as a `PARTIAL PASS / STATISTICAL TIE` (`p = 0.8237`).

## 16. Round 25: `GLC_AOM_v3` In-Network Observable Syndrome Attention + Symmetric Propagator Synergy Curve ($k \in \{0, 1, 2, 4, 8\}$) & `Kyle-AMM` Collinearity-Damped Price Clearing (`M4_v3` / `M5_v3`) on `NVIDIA L4` GPU (`846.88s`, `[R25:1-152, JR25, PR25]`)

### 16.1 `R25-E1` `GLC_AOM_v3` In-Network Observable Syndrome Attention & Full Symmetric Propagator Curve ($k \in \{0, 1, 2, 4, 8\}$ Across 8 Parameter-Matched `1.20M` Models, `846.88s` on `NVIDIA L4` GPU, `[R25:28-102, JR25]`)

Wave-2 (`R22B-E1`, `Section 13.1`) revealed two concrete bottlenecks on public reasoning benchmarks: (i) at `k=0` (`Track A` Pure Neural), `GLC_AOM_v2` (`1,199,937` params) ranked `#1` macro-average (`31.11%`) yet hit `0.00%` on `SATLIB 3-SAT (n=50 OOD)` and `13.89%` on `Sokoban` because its variable tokens did not compute explicit bipartite variable-to-check syndrome messages inside the neural forward pass; and (ii) `Track B` tested only $k=1$ external unit-propagation pass (`31.39%` vs. `31.11%` at $k=0$), leaving open how neural search and $k$-pass observable constraint propagation synergize across $k \in \{0, 1, 2, 4, 8\}$.

In **Round 25 (`R25-E1`)**, we designed **`GLC_AOM_v3` (`GLCAOMv3SyndromeSolver`, `1,200,018` params — matched within `0.0068%` of `1,199,968` across all 8 paradigms)**, which computes differentiable bipartite variable-check syndrome residuals inside its recurrent layers without calling any external helper (`helper_calls = 0` at `k=0`). Executed across 3 seeds (`101, 102, 103`) on an **`NVIDIA L4` GPU** (`846.88s` total wall-clock for `R25-E1 + R25-E2`, `0` AST `v2` violations across `4,142` lines of [`benchmarks/run_r25_v3_reasoning_and_kyle.py`](../../benchmarks/run_r25_v3_reasoning_and_kyle.py), `11/11` combined AST + Runtime canaries caught, `100%` live pass on `S11=True, S12=True, S13=True, S14=True` across all 3 seeds):
- **`Track A` (`k=0` Pure Neural, Zero Helper Calls, Table 36):** `GLC_AOM_v3` strictly improves over `GLC_AOM_v2` on **all `5/5` tasks** at $B=64$, lifting 5-task macro solve rate by **`+24.72 pp` from `31.11%` to `55.83%` (`#1` of 8 overall** vs. `30.00%` / `32.22%` `MDLM`, `21.11%` / `20.56%` `Searchformer`, `19.72%` / `20.28%` `Stream_of_Search`, `17.78%` / `17.50%` `AR_GRPO`, `15.56%` / `18.89%` `BoN_Verifier`, `11.11%` / `10.83%` `TRM_Looped`). `GLC_AOM_v3` wins **`#1` on `sudoku_extreme_81` (`26.39% ± 8.56%` vs. `18.06% ± 7.08%` `v2` and `0.00%` for all 6 external baselines)**, **`#1` on `sokoban_10` (`70.83% ± 11.79%` vs. `5.56%` `v2` and `18.06%` `AR_GRPO`, `+52.77 pp`)**, and **`#1` on `countdown_6` (`98.61% ± 1.96%` vs. `95.83%` `Searchformer` and `91.67%` `v2`)**, while jumping **`+25.00 pp` on `satlib_3sat_20` (`40.28% -> 65.28% ± 7.86%`, `#2` behind `MDLM` `73.61% ± 10.94%`)** and **`+18.06 pp` on `satlib_3sat_50_ood` (`0.00% -> 18.06% ± 7.08%`, `#2` behind `MDLM` `44.44% ± 8.56%`, while all other 6 models score `0.00%`)**.
- **Full Symmetric Propagator Synergy Curve $k \in \{0, 1, 2, 4, 8\}$ (`S13` Spread `= 0`, Table 37):** `Track 0` (`0` neural forward calls + $k$ symmetric propagator passes) solves **`0.00% ± 0.00%`** across all $k \in \{0, 1, 2, 4\}$ (`0/360` instances; `57.03 -> 45.76 -> 45.16 -> 44.25` violations) and `0.56% ± 0.79%` (`2/360` instances, `2.78%` on `sudoku_extreme_81`) at extreme $k=8$. Across all 8 neural paradigms, **`GLC_AOM_v3` ranks `#1` in 5-task macro solve rate at every single propagator depth $k \in \{0, 1, 2, 4, 8\}$**: **`55.83%` ($k=0$) $\to$ `56.94%` ($k=1$) $\to$ `58.06%` ($k=2$) $\to$ `58.06%` ($k=4$) $\to$ `61.11%` ($k=8$)**, reaching **`43.06% ± 8.56%` on `sudoku_extreme_81` at $k=8$** and **`26.39% ± 5.20%` on `satlib_3sat_50_ood` at $k=2$**.

### 16.2 `R25-E2` `Kyle-AMM` Collinearity-Damped Price Clearing (`M4_v3` & `M5_v3`) Resolving Decentralized Market Logit Inflation (`0` Cross-Module Autograd Edges, `[R25:105-152, JR25]`)

In Wave-2 (`R23-E2`, `Section 14.2`), decentralized `Kyle-LMSR` (`M4`) and `Futarchy` (`M5`) achieved `#1` unobserved test accuracy across all 3 real environments (`sudoku_6x6`, `equiv_sat`, `micro_stratego_4x4`) with `0` cross-module autograd edges, yet exhibited elevated posterior KL (`3.6923`–`3.9989` on `sudoku_6x6` and `3.7721`–`3.7968` on `micro_stratego_4x4` in `R23-E2`; `5.2463`–`9.1143` in uncalibrated high-step `M4_v2`/`M5_v2`) because sequential `Kyle-AMM` accumulated raw additive log-odds trades $\Delta \ell_m$ without discounting collinear order-flow crowding across modules or normalizing market-clearing temperature.

To resolve this from first principles while preserving **`0` cross-module autograd edges (`grad_fn is None` on every broadcast market price tensor)** and **100% live counterfactual `S14` pass**, **`M4_v3_Kyle_Collinearity_Damped_LIR` and `M5_v3_Kyle_Futarchy_Evolution`** (`[R25:105-152]`) introduce:
1. **Pre-Entry Order-Flow Collinearity Damping ($d_{\text{crowd}, m}$):** Before module $m$ submits its net trade innovation $\delta_m$, the AMM computes its positive cosine crowding $d_{\text{crowd}, m} = 1 + \sum_{j < m} \max(0, \cos(\delta_m, \delta_j))$ against already-cleared order flow (`mean_d_crowd = 5.47`–`6.94` for `M4_v3` and `8.22`–`8.66` for `M5_v3`) and scales effective price impact as $\Delta P_m = (\lambda_m / (b_{\text{liq}} \cdot d_{\text{crowd}, m})) \delta_m$.
2. **Order-Book RMS Normalization & Liquidity Temperature Calibration ($\tau_{\text{clear}}^*$):** Fits a single scalar clearing temperature $\tau_{\text{clear}}^*$ on a held-out calibration split (`0.550` on `sudoku_6x6`, `1.467`–`1.583` on `equiv_sat`, `1.400`–`1.467` on `micro_stratego_4x4`).

As Table 38 shows, **`M5_v3_Kyle_Futarchy_Evolution` slashes uncalibrated `M4_v2`/`M5_v2` Posterior KL by `87.9%` on `sudoku_6x6` (`9.1143 -> 1.1041`), `96.8%` on `equiv_sat` (`5.5586 -> 0.1780`), and `95.9%` on `micro_stratego_4x4` (`7.5698 -> 0.3138`), and beats centralized `M1_Global_Backprop_Oracle` (`66` cross-module grad edges) on Accuracy, Posterior KL, AND Brier Score across ALL THREE real environments**:
- **`sudoku_6x6`:** `M5_v3` achieves **`63.69% ± 5.90%` (`#1`)** vs. `50.06% ± 2.49%` `M1_Global_Backprop_Oracle` (**`+13.63 pp`**), Posterior KL **`1.1041 ± 0.0412` (`#1`)** < `1.1650 ± 0.0791` `M1` < `1.2880` `M4_v3` < `1.3603` `M3` < `1.4987` `M2` $\ll$ `8.7227` `M5_v2`, and Brier **`0.6081` (`#1`)** < `0.6601` `M1`.
- **`equiv_sat`:** `M5_v3` achieves **`61.26% ± 2.67%` (`#1`)** vs. `55.56% ± 2.47%` `M1` (**`+5.70 pp`**), Posterior KL **`0.1780 ± 0.0256` (`#1`)** < **`0.2344 ± 0.0070` `M4_v3`** < `0.2989` `M2` < `0.5324 ± 0.0774` `M1` $\ll$ `5.2463` `M5_v2`, and Brier **`0.4411` (`#1`)** < `0.4931` `M4_v3` < `0.6278` `M1`.
- **`micro_stratego_4x4`:** `M5_v3` achieves **`37.47% ± 5.55%` (`#1`)** > **`37.29% ± 1.71%` `M4_v3`** > `36.19% ± 1.98%` `M3` > `35.47% ± 2.71%` `M2` > `32.36% ± 0.46%` `M1` (**`+5.11 pp`**), Posterior KL **`0.3138 ± 0.0038` (`#1`)** < **`0.3403 ± 0.0106` `M4_v3`** < `0.3705` `M2` < `0.6547 ± 0.0764` `M1` $\ll$ `7.7739` `M5_v2`, and Brier **`0.7185` (`#1`)** < `0.7288` `M4_v3` < `0.9013` `M1`.
